```
# SPEC — Covertree property records

Source: Covertree "AI Software Engineer" assessment brief. This spec is the contract the
`reviewer` agent checks against; story IDs (`S1`…`S7`) and criterion IDs (`S3.2`) are stable
— reference them in commits, tests and reviews.

## Problem statement

Covertree needs to manage records of US residential properties through a GraphQL API with a
React UI. Each property must be enriched with the current weather and coordinates of its
location at the moment it is created, so records carry location data the user never has to
enter by hand.

## Goals

1. Every user story S1–S7 is implemented end to end (API + UI) and covered by automated tests.
2. Weatherstack is called **only** inside the `createProperty` mutation — never on reads, never
   in tests.
3. A reviewer can run the whole app from the README in ≤ 3 commands (plus `.env`).
4. The GraphQL schema is the single contract: server resolvers and client documents are typed
   from it by codegen, with zero hand-written duplicate types.

## Non-goals

- **Authentication / multi-tenancy** — not in the brief; every user sees all properties.
- **Updating a property** — the brief lists create/read/delete only; weather is a creation
  snapshot, so edits would make it misleading.
- **Refreshing weather after creation** — the brief forbids calling Weatherstack outside creation.
- **Address autocomplete / geocoding services** — lat/long come from the Weatherstack response.
- **Pagination** — P2; the dataset in scope is small. The list API is shaped so it can be added.
- **Non-US addresses** — the brief assumes all properties are in the United States.

## User stories

… 17 more lines truncated (3046 chars total)
```

union CreatePropertyResult =
  | CreatePropertySuccess
  | WeatherUnavailableError
  | InvalidInputError

type WeatherUnavailableError {
  message: String!
  reason: WeatherFailureReason!

  UPSTREAM_ERROR  # success:false
  TIMEOUT
  NETWORK
  INVALID_RESPONSE

```
# SPEC — Covertree property records

Source: Covertree "AI Software Engineer" assessment brief plus product decisions recorded
below. This spec is the contract the `reviewer` agent checks against; story IDs (`S1`…`S7`)
and criterion IDs (`S3.2`) are stable — reference them in commits, tests and reviews.

## Problem statement

Covertree needs to manage records of US residential properties through a GraphQL API with a
React UI. Each property must be enriched with the current weather and coordinates of its
location at the moment it is created, so records carry location data the user never has to
enter by hand. A property without that data is incomplete, so it must never be stored.

## Goals

1. Every user story S1–S7 is implemented end to end (API + UI) and covered by automated tests
   named after the criteria below.
2. Weatherstack is called **only** inside the `createProperty` mutation — never on reads,
   deletes, or in tests.
3. No property is ever persisted without Weatherstack weather, `lat` and `long`; every failure
   mode reaches the client as a **typed** error in the schema.
4. The GraphQL schema is the single contract: server resolvers and client documents are typed
   from it by codegen, with zero hand-written duplicate types.
5. A reviewer can run the whole app from the README in ≤ 3 commands (plus `.env`).

## Non-goals

- **Authentication / multi-tenancy** — not in the brief; every user sees all properties.
- **Updating a property** — the brief lists create/read/delete only; weather is a creation
  snapshot, so edits would make it misleading.
- **Refreshing weather after creation** — the brief forbids calling Weatherstack outside creation.
- **Address autocomplete / geocoding services** — lat/long come from the Weatherstack response.
- **Pagination** — P2; the dataset in scope is small. The list API takes input objects so it
  can be added without breaking the contract.
- **Non-US addresses** — the brief assumes all properties are in the United States.

## Decisions

| Topic                     | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
… 231 more lines truncated (18515 chars total)
```

I've rewritten `docs/SPEC.md` using the brief and your answers. Nothing is committed.

**What's now fixed in the spec** (all in a new "Decisions" table, each backed by numbered criteria):
- **Sort:** newest first by default (S2.1). Ties are broken by `id` so the order is always the same (S2.3).
- **Filters:**
  - All filter fields are optional and combine with AND. A missing, null or blank field means no filter on it (S3.1, S3.4, S3.9).
  - City matches as a case-insensitive substring. `%` and `_` are treated as plain characters (S3.2, S3.3).
  - Zip must be exactly 5 digits and matches exactly. A bad zip is rejected with an error, not an empty list (S3.5, S3.6).
  - State matches the enum value exactly (S3.7).
- **Validation:** allowed states are the 50 states, DC and five territories (56 values). Zip must match `^\d{5}$`. Street and city are trimmed and length-checked. All invalid fields are reported together, before Weatherstack is called (S5.3).
- **Weatherstack failure:** `createProperty` returns a result union (success, invalid input, duplicate, or weather unavailable). On any failure it returns `WeatherUnavailableError` and saves nothing. There is one `reason` value per failure type: `NETWORK`, `TIMEOUT`, `HTTP_ERROR`, `UPSTREAM_ERROR`, `INVALID_RESPONSE`, `LOCATION_MISMATCH` (S5.5). There is one request, with a 5 s timeout and no retries.
- **Country check:** the country Weatherstack returns must match an allowed list for the input state (S5.6).
- **Duplicates:** rejected before Weatherstack is called. Two records are duplicates only if all four fields match exactly after trimming, so case differences count as different addresses. A database unique constraint handles two identical creates at the same time (S5.4).
- **Delete:** returns a union: success or `PropertyNotFoundError` (S6.2, S6.3).

**Choices I made without asking** — tell me if you want any changed:
- For the list and detail queries, a bad argument returns a normal GraphQL error with code `BAD_USER_INPUT`. Only mutations return result unions.
- An unknown or malformed id is treated the same way: `property` returns `null`, and delete returns `PropertyNotFoundError`.
- `HTTP_ERROR` and `LOCATION_MISMATCH` are added to the four failure reasons you picked.
- e2e (X4) now also covers a Weatherstack failure: the UI shows the weather error and the list doesn't change.

**Still open, to check on the first real Weatherstack call:**
- The exact country names Weatherstack returns for territories (the S5.6 table is provisional).
- Whether the query `"<zip>, <state>, USA"` works for territory zips like `00901, PR, USA`.

**Other files changed:** the new spec renumbered some criteria and switched to result unions, so I updated the references in `CLAUDE.md`, the `reviewer` agent, the `graphql-slice` and `verify` skills, and the `graphql-slice` evals. The `graphql-slice` skill told resolvers to throw coded `GraphQLError`s, which contradicted the unions. The session log entry in `AI_WORKFLOW.md` still cites the old IDs; I left it alone because it's a historical record.

If this looks right, a suggested commit is `docs: SPEC decisions for sort, filters, validation and typed Weatherstack errors`.

````
### Define Prisma schema with prisma-client generator in schema.prisma

Source: https://github.com/prisma/web/blob/main/apps/blog/content/blog/testing-series-1-8eRB5p0Y8o/index.mdx

Configures the Prisma schema using generator client provider 'prisma-client' with a custom output path and PostgreSQL datasource provider without a hardcoded URL.

```prisma
// prisma/schema.prisma
generator client {
  provider = "prisma-client"
  output   = "../src/generated/prisma"
}

datasource db {
  provider = "postgresql"
}

model User {
  id    Int     @id @default(autoincrement())
  email String? @unique
  name  String?
  posts Post[]
}

model Post {
  id        Int     @id @default(autoincrement())
  title     String
  content   String?
  published Boolean @default(false)
  author    User    @relation(fields: [authorId], references: [id])
  authorId  Int
}
```
````

````
### Specify custom constraint and index names using map attributes

Source: https://github.com/prisma/web/blob/main/apps/docs/content/docs/guides/upgrade-prisma-orm/v3.mdx

Set database-level names for primary keys, unique constraints, and foreign keys using the `map` argument.

```prisma
model User {
  id    Int    @id(map: "Custom_Primary_Key") @default(autoincrement())
  email String @unique(map: "Custom_Unique_Constraint")
  posts Post[]
}

model Post {
  id        Int    @id @default(autoincrement())
  title     String
  author    User   @relation(fields: [authorId], references: [id], map: "Custom_Foreign_Key")
  authorId  Int
}
```
````

I wrote both files and haven't generated anything from them yet. The GraphQL SDL passes `graphql-js` validation, and both files list the same 56 `USState` values. The Prisma schema hasn't been checked by Prisma, because Prisma isn't installed yet.

I put the SDL at `apps/api/src/schema.graphql`, not `apps/api/source/…`, because CLAUDE.md and the harness expect `src/`.

**`apps/api/src/schema.graphql`** (descriptions and the 56 enum values left out here):

```graphql
scalar DateTime
enum USState { AL AK AZ … WY DC PR GU VI AS MP }   # 56
enum SortDirection { ASC DESC }

type Property {
  id: ID!
  street: String!
  city: String!
  state: USState!
  zipCode: String!
  weatherData: Weather!
  lat: Float!
  long: Float!
  createdAt: DateTime!
}

type Weather {                       # Weatherstack `current`, °F / mph
  observationTime: String!  temperature: Float!  feelsLike: Float!
  weatherDescriptions: [String!]!  weatherIcons: [String!]!
  humidity: Int!  windSpeed: Float!  windDir: String!       # S4.4 fields: non-null
  weatherCode: Int  windDegree: Int  pressure: Float  precip: Float
  cloudcover: Int  uvIndex: Float  visibility: Float  isDay: Boolean   # others nullable
}

input PropertyFilter  { city: String, zipCode: String, state: USState }
input PropertyOrderBy { createdAt: SortDirection! = DESC }

type Query {
  properties(filter: PropertyFilter, orderBy: PropertyOrderBy): [Property!]!
  property(id: ID!): Property
}

input CreatePropertyInput { street: String!, city: String!, state: USState!, zipCode: String! }

union CreatePropertyResult = CreatePropertySuccess | InvalidInputError
                           | DuplicatePropertyError | WeatherUnavailableError
type CreatePropertySuccess   { property: Property! }
type InvalidInputError       { message: String!, fieldErrors: [FieldError!]! }
type FieldError              { field: String!, message: String! }
type DuplicatePropertyError  { message: String!, existingPropertyId: ID! }
type WeatherUnavailableError { message: String!, reason: WeatherFailureReason! }
enum WeatherFailureReason { NETWORK TIMEOUT HTTP_ERROR UPSTREAM_ERROR INVALID_RESPONSE LOCATION_MISMATCH }

union DeletePropertyResult = DeletePropertySuccess | PropertyNotFoundError
type DeletePropertySuccess { id: ID! }
type PropertyNotFoundError { message: String!, id: ID! }

type Mutation {
  createProperty(input: CreatePropertyInput!): CreatePropertyResult!
  deleteProperty(id: ID!): DeletePropertyResult!
}
```

**`apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma`**:

```prisma
generator client {
  provider = "prisma-client"            // Prisma 7; output is now required
  output   = "../src/generated/prisma"
}
datasource db { provider = "postgresql" }  // URL goes in prisma.config.ts (Prisma 7)

enum USState { AL AK … MP }   // same 56 values as the GraphQL enum

model Property {
  id          String   @id @default(dbgenerated("gen_random_uuid()")) @db.Uuid
  street      String   @db.VarChar(200)
  city        String   @db.VarChar(100)
  state       USState
  zipCode     String   @map("zip_code") @db.Char(5)
  weatherData Json     @map("weather_data")        // jsonb, raw Weatherstack `current`
  lat         Float
  long        Float
  createdAt   DateTime @default(now()) @map("created_at") @db.Timestamptz(3)

  @@unique([street, city, state, zipCode])   // S5.4 duplicate rule
  @@index([createdAt, id])                   // S2 sort + id tiebreak
  @@index([city])
  @@index([state])
  @@index([zipCode])
  @@map("properties")
}
```

**Decisions and one problem you should know about:**
- **The `city` index won't be used by the filter.** The SPEC's case-insensitive substring match compiles to `ILIKE '%…%'`, and a plain index can't serve that. I kept `@@index([city])` as you asked. The fix is a trigram index, which means enabling Postgres's `pg_trgm` extension in a migration, and I'd recommend it. It's harmless at this data size, so it can wait.
- **The `createdAt` index is `(createdAt, id)`.** It serves both sort directions and the S2.3 tiebreak on `id`.
- **`weatherData`** is stored as JSON with Weatherstack's snake_case keys and exposed as the typed `Weather` object, as you chose. The API will convert the keys to camelCase.
- **`USState`** is also a Prisma enum, so the database rejects bad states itself. Codegen will be set to reuse Prisma's enum so the two can't drift apart.

Should I trade `@@index([city])` for the trigram index, or keep it as it is? Once you're happy with the schemas, the next step is scaffolding `apps/api` so codegen and the first migration can run.

```
# GraphQL slice

Build one story from `docs/SPEC.md` so it works end to end and is provably done. The order below
matters. The schema is the contract, so types flow from it to every layer. Tests are named after
SPEC criteria, so the reviewer agent can check each one.

The architecture and testing rules live in `CLAUDE.md` (layering, Weatherstack injection, no real
network in tests, never edit generated files). Follow them rather than re-deriving them. The ESLint
layering rule and the hooks enforce them anyway.

## 0. Frame the slice

1. Read the story and **every** criterion ID for it in `docs/SPEC.md`, plus `X1`–`X4`.
2. List the criteria you will satisfy, and note which layer and which test proves each one. Show
   the user this short plan before coding.
3. If a criterion is ambiguous, or conflicts with the code, stop and ask. Don't silently change
   behavior the SPEC defines. If the user agrees to a change, update `docs/SPEC.md` in the same
   slice.

## 1. Scaffold (first slice only)

If `apps/api` or `apps/web` doesn't exist yet, scaffold the missing app(s) first, following
[references/scaffold.md](references/scaffold.md). Scaffold only what this slice needs plus the
test harness. Look up current versions and APIs with **context7** first. Prisma, Apollo Client and
codegen presets have changed shape across recent majors, and stale memory produces broken config.
```

````
Integration tests (`*.int.test.ts`) need postgres-test. Run `prisma migrate reset --force` against
`TEST_DATABASE_URL` in a globalSetup, and truncate between tests.
````

````
### Configure PostgreSQL extensions in Prisma schema

Source: https://github.com/prisma/web/blob/main/apps/blog/content/blog/wnip-q4-2022-f66prwkjx72s/index.mdx

Enable the postgresqlExtensions preview feature in the generator block to define and manage database extensions via the extensions property in the datasource block.

```jsx
generator client {
  provider        = "prisma-client-js"
  previewFeatures = ["postgresqlExtensions"]
}
datasource db {
  provider   = "postgresql"
  url        = env("DATABASE_URL")
  extensions = [hstore(schema: "myHstoreSchema"), pg_tgrm, postgis(version: "2.1")]
}
```

--------------------------------

### PostgreSQL index recommendations for case-insensitive contains

Source: https://github.com/prisma/web/blob/main/apps/docs/content/docs/orm/v6/prisma-client/queries/case-sensitivity.mdx

Recommends pg_trgm trigram-based GIN indexes to accelerate contains/startsWith/endsWith queries that use LIKE/ILIKE in PostgreSQL.

```text
If you rely heavily on case-insensitive filtering, consider [creating indexes in the PostgreSQL database](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/indexes.html) to improve performance:

- [Create an expression index](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/indexes-expressional.html) for Prisma Client queries that use `equals` or `not`
- Use the `pg_trgm` module to [create a trigram-based index](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/12/pgtrgm.html#id-1.11.7.40.7) for Prisma Client queries that use `startsWith`, `endsWith`, `contains` (maps to`LIKE` / `ILIKE` in PostgreSQL)
```
````

````
### Configure GIN index in PostgreSQL

Source: https://github.com/prisma/web/blob/main/apps/blog/content/blog/wnip-q2-2022-pmn7rulcj8x/index.mdx

Applies a GIN index to a Json column in PostgreSQL using the @@index attribute. GiST, BRIN, and SP-GiST are alternative supported index types.

```prisma
datasource db {
  provider = "postgresql"
  url      = env("DATABASE_URL")
}

model Post {
  id      Int     @id
  title   String
  content String?
  tags    Json?

  @@index([tags], type: Gin) // alternatives: GiST, BRIN, and SP-GiST
}
```

--------------------------------

### Enable pg_trgm extension and create GIN trigram index

Source: https://github.com/prisma/web/blob/main/apps/blog/content/blog/you-dont-need-elasticsearch-postgres-already-has-full-text-search/index.mdx

Enables the pg_trgm extension and creates a GIN index with gin_trgm_ops to accelerate trigram similarity queries.

```sql
CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm;
CREATE INDEX articles_title_trgm_idx ON articles USING GIN (title gin_trgm_ops);
```
````

````
### Extend GraphQL Context with a Function

Source: https://github.com/graphql-hive/graphql-yoga/blob/main/website/src/content/docs/features/context.mdx

Use a function for the `context` property in `createYoga` to dynamically generate context for each request. The function receives the initial context and its return value is merged.

```typescript
import { createServer } from 'http'
import { createSchema, createYoga } from 'graphql-yoga'

const yoga = createYoga({
  schema: createSchema({
    typeDefs: /* GraphQL */ `
      type Query {
        someNumber: Int!
      }
    `,
    resolvers: {
      Query: {
        someNumber(_, _args, context) {
          return context.someNumber
        }
      }
    }
  }),
  context() {
    return { someNumber: 13 }
  }
})

const server = createServer(yoga)
server.listen(4000, () => {
  console.info('Server is running on http://localhost:4000/graphql')
})
```

--------------------------------
````

````
### Codegen Configuration with Custom Type Mappers

Source: https://github.com/dotansimha/graphql-code-generator/blob/master/website/src/pages/plugins/typescript/typescript-resolvers.mdx

Configure `graphql-codegen` to map GraphQL types to your custom model types using the `mappers` option. This allows you to use your own defined types for resolvers and context.

```typescript
import type { CodegenConfig } from '@graphql-codegen/cli'

const config: CodegenConfig = {
  schema: 'schema.graphql',
  generates: {
    './resolvers-types.ts': {
      config: {
        contextType: 'models#MyContextType',
        mappers: {
          User: './models#UserModel',
          Profile: './models#UserProfile'
        }
      },
      plugins: ['typescript', 'typescript-resolvers']
    }
  }
}
```
````

````
### Instantiate Prisma Client with driver adapter in src/lib/prisma.ts

Source: https://github.com/prisma/web/blob/main/apps/blog/content/blog/testing-series-1-8eRB5p0Y8o/index.mdx

Initializes PrismaClient in Prisma 7 using the PrismaPg driver adapter and environment variables loaded via dotenv.

```ts
// src/lib/prisma.ts
import 'dotenv/config'
import { PrismaPg } from '@prisma/adapter-pg'
import { PrismaClient } from '../generated/prisma/client'

const adapter = new PrismaPg({ connectionString: process.env.DATABASE_URL! })
const prisma = new PrismaClient({ adapter })

export default prisma
```

--------------------------------

### Instantiate PrismaClient with @prisma/adapter-pg (After)

Source: https://github.com/prisma/web/blob/main/apps/docs/content/docs/guides/upgrade-prisma-orm/v7.mdx

Creates a PrismaClient instance using the PrismaPg driver adapter for PostgreSQL databases.

```ts
import { PrismaClient } from "./generated/prisma/client";
import { PrismaPg } from "@prisma/adapter-pg";

const adapter = new PrismaPg({
  connectionString: process.env.DATABASE_URL,
});
export const prisma = new PrismaClient({ adapter });
```
````

**🔧 Write** `~/Projects/covertree-properties/apps/api/prisma.config.ts`

```
import { defineConfig } from 'prisma/config';
import { loadRootEnv } from './src/load-env.ts';

loadRootEnv();

export default defineConfig({
  schema: 'prisma/schema.prisma',
  migrations: { path: 'prisma/migrations' },
  // Undefined is fine for `prisma generate`; migrate commands fail with a clear message.
  datasource: { url: process.env['DATABASE_URL'] },
});

```

**🔧 Write** `~/Projects/covertree-properties/apps/api/src/load-env.ts`

```
/** Loads the repo-root `.env` if present. Variables already set in the environment win. */
export function loadRootEnv(): void {
  try {
    process.loadEnvFile(new URL('../../../.env', import.meta.url));
  } catch (error) {
    if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== 'ENOENT') throw error;
  }
}

```

````
### Define Project Names in Vitest Config

Source: https://main.vitest.dev/api/advanced/test-project

Configure project names in Vitest by specifying paths to packages with package.json or by directly providing project configurations. Vitest resolves names from package.json or folder names if not explicitly set.

```javascript
import { createVitest } from 'vitest/node'

const vitest = await createVitest('test')
vitest.projects.map(p => p.name) === [
  '@pkg/server',
  'utils',
  '2',
  'custom'
]
```

```javascript
import { defineConfig } from 'vitest/config'

export default defineConfig({
  test: {
    projects: [
      './packages/server', // has package.json with "@pkg/server"
      './utils', // doesn't have a package.json file
      {
        // doesn't customize the name
        test: {
          pool: 'threads',
        },
      },
      {
        // customized the name

          name: 'custom',

      },
````

**🔧 Bash** — Switch integration setup to migrate deploy + truncate, run tests

```bash
cd ~/Projects/covertree-properties/apps/api
cat > test/global-setup.int.ts <<'EOF'
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { TEST_DATABASE_URL } from './test-database-url.ts';

/** Applies pending migrations to postgres-test once per integration run (never drops data). */
export function setup(): void {
  const url = TEST_DATABASE_URL;
  if (!url.includes(':5433/')) throw new Error(`Refusing to migrate a non-test database: ${url}`);
  execFileSync('pnpm', ['exec', 'prisma', 'migrate', 'deploy'], {
    env: { ...process.env, DATABASE_URL: url },
    stdio: 'inherit',
  });
}
EOF
cat > test/database.ts <<'EOF'
import { createPrismaClient } from '../src/db.ts';
import { TEST_DATABASE_URL } from './test-database-url.ts';

/** Prisma client for integration tests, bound to postgres-test. */
export const testPrisma = createPrismaClient(TEST_DATABASE_URL);

/** Empties every table; call in `beforeEach` so tests don't see each other's rows. */
export async function resetTables(): Promise<void> {
  await testPrisma.$executeRaw`TRUNCATE TABLE "properties"`;
}
EOF
cat > test/db.int.test.ts <<'EOF'
import { afterAll, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { resetTables, testPrisma } from './database.ts';

describe('database scaffold', () => {
  beforeEach(resetTables);
  afterAll(() => testPrisma.$disconnect());

    await expect(testPrisma.property.count()).resolves.toBe(0);
    const extensions = await testPrisma.$queryRaw<{ extname: string }[]>`
      SELECT extname FROM pg_extension WHERE extname = 'pg_trgm'`;
    expect(extensions).toHaveLength(1);
```

`apps/api` is scaffolded with the trigram index in place, and `pnpm verify` passes from the repo root: format, lint, typecheck, unit + integration tests, and build. The e2e step is still empty. Nothing is committed yet.

**Trigram index**
- The plain `@@index([city])` is now a trigram GIN index on `city`, which can serve the case-insensitive substring filter.
- Prisma can declare the index but not the `pg_trgm` extension it needs, so the migration creates the extension before the table.
- The migration `init_property` is applied to the dev database, and `migrate diff` shows no drift.

**What the scaffold contains**
- **Wiring:** Yoga server on `node:http`, a Prisma 7 client, env checked with Zod, and GraphQL types generated from `schema.graphql`.
- **Resolvers and services:** only the `DateTime` scalar so far. The `properties`, `property` and mutation fields have no resolvers yet; the story slices add them along with their services. The Weatherstack client comes with S5.
- **Tests:** two Vitest projects. `unit` gets MSW with `onUnhandledRequest: 'error'` and a fake key. `integration` runs against `postgres-test` and refuses to start against any other database. There's one smoke test in each: the API serves the schema, and migrations plus `pg_trgm` are applied.
- **Generated files:** everything under `__generated__/` is gitignored and rebuilt by a `postinstall` script.
- **Check:** I also built it and started the compiled server; it answered GraphQL requests.

**Changes to earlier decisions**
- **Prisma version:** Prisma's `latest` tag points at `8.0.0-rc.15`, a prerelease. The CLI is pinned to `7.10.0` to match the client.
- **Build scripts:** `pnpm-workspace.yaml` now lets Prisma and esbuild run their install scripts; msw's is left off.
- **Prisma output path:** the generated client now lives in `src/__generated__/prisma`, and the ESLint layering rule was updated to match.
- **Test database setup:** as you chose, integration tests now apply migrations with `migrate deploy` and clear the table between tests. I updated the graphql-slice scaffold reference so later slices don't bring back `migrate reset`.

**Before you run it:** there's no `.env` yet, so do `cp .env.example .env` and add your Weatherstack key. The README "Run it" section is still missing; it belongs to X3, and I'll add it once the web app exists.

```
# ADR-0002: GraphQL Yoga over Apollo Server

**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-09-24
**Deciders:** repo owner (reviewed with Claude Code, see `ai/sessions/`)

## Context

The API is SDL-first ([ADR-0001](0001-stack.md)): `schema.graphql` plus resolvers typed by
GraphQL Code Generator. We need a server that runs that schema on Node, lets tests inject a fake
Weatherstack client through the context (SPEC X1, X2), and can be exercised in tests without
opening a port. There is one schema, no federation, and no hosted observability requirement.

## Decision

Use **GraphQL Yoga** (`createYoga` + `createSchema`) on plain `node:http`.

## Options considered

### Option A: GraphQL Yoga (chosen)

| Dimension        | Assessment                                                                   |
| ---------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Complexity       | Low: one package, works directly as a `node:http` request handler            |
| Testability      | `yoga.fetch(request)` runs a full HTTP round trip in-process                 |
| Fit with codegen | `createSchema<Context>({ typeDefs, resolvers })` takes the generated types   |
| Ecosystem        | Envelop plugins; smaller community than Apollo's                             |

**Pros:** built on the Fetch API, so the same app runs in tests, on Node, or on edge runtimes.
Few dependencies. GraphiQL is built in. Supports `graphql@17`, which this repo uses.
**Cons:** less familiar to reviewers than Apollo; no first-party hosted metrics.

### Option B: Apollo Server

| Dimension        | Assessment                                                                       |
| ---------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Complexity       | Low–Med: `startStandaloneServer`, or a framework integration package             |
| Testability      | `server.executeOperation` skips the HTTP layer; HTTP tests need a running server |
| Fit with codegen | Equally good (`typescript-resolvers` targets both)                               |
| Ecosystem        | Largest; GraphOS usage reporting, federation                                     |
… 26 more lines truncated (3428 chars total)
```

```
# ADR-0003: Prisma over Drizzle

**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-09-24
**Deciders:** repo owner (reviewed with Claude Code, see `ai/sessions/`)

## Context

One table (`properties`) in PostgreSQL ([ADR-0001](0001-stack.md)). Needs: typed queries for a
case-insensitive substring filter plus sort (SPEC S2, S3), a duplicate unique constraint (S5.4),
JSON storage for `weatherData`, versioned migrations a reviewer can apply with one command, and a
clean repository layer that is the only DB-aware code (X1).

## Decision

Use **Prisma ORM 7** (`prisma-client` generator, `@prisma/adapter-pg`), with migrations
committed under `apps/api/prisma/migrations`.

## Options considered

### Option A: Prisma (chosen)

| Dimension          | Assessment                                                                   |
| ------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Complexity         | Med: separate schema language, `prisma generate`, `prisma.config.ts`         |
| Query ergonomics   | `contains` + `mode: 'insensitive'` expresses S3.2 directly                   |
| Migrations         | `migrate dev/deploy`, drift detection via `migrate diff`                     |
| Reviewer knowledge | High; the most widely known TypeScript ORM                                   |

**Pros:** declarative schema that reads like the SPEC's S7 table; generated types act as the
repository's return types; mature migrations with drift detection.
**Cons:**
- A generate step (automated by a Claude Code hook and `postinstall`).
- Postgres extensions and operator classes need a hand edit: the `pg_trgm` GIN index declares
  `ops: raw(...)`, and the migration adds `CREATE EXTENSION`.
- Prisma 7 needs a driver adapter.
- The CLI blocks `migrate reset` when an AI agent runs it, so tests use `migrate deploy` + `TRUNCATE`.
- The `latest` npm tag currently points to an 8.0 RC, so versions are pinned.

### Option B: Drizzle ORM
```

```
# ADR-0004: Weather failure policy: fail creation with a typed error

**Status:** Accepted
**Date:** 2026-09-24
**Deciders:** repo owner (reviewed with Claude Code, see `ai/sessions/`)

## Context

`createProperty` must call Weatherstack `/current` (the brief allows it only there) to get
`weatherData`, `lat` and `long`. Weatherstack can fail in many ways: network, timeout, non-2xx,
**HTTP 200 with `{ success: false }`** (bad key, quota, unknown location), a malformed body, or a
resolved location outside the US. `lat`/`long` exist only in that response. The product owner's
requirement: without Weatherstack data, creation **fails with an explicit error**.

## Decision

Weatherstack failure **aborts the mutation**. It makes one request (5 s timeout, no retries) and
persists nothing. It returns `WeatherUnavailableError { message, reason }` as a member of the
`CreatePropertyResult` union, where `reason` is a `WeatherFailureReason` enum: `NETWORK`,
`TIMEOUT`, `HTTP_ERROR`, `UPSTREAM_ERROR`, `INVALID_RESPONSE`, `LOCATION_MISMATCH` (SPEC
S5.5–S5.6). Validation and the duplicate check run before the call, so bad input never spends quota.

## Options considered

| Option                                               | Data integrity                          | Client handling                         | Complexity            |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | --------------------- |
| **A. Fail, typed union error (chosen)**              | Every row complete (all columns non-null) | Typed `switch` on `__typename`/`reason` | Low                   |
| B. Fail, `errors[]` + `extensions.code`              | Same                                    | Untyped; string-matching codes          | Lowest                |
| C. Persist without weather, mark `weatherStatus`     | Partial rows; lat/long null             | Every reader handles "no weather"       | Med                   |
| D. Persist, then enrich async with retries           | Eventually complete                     | Pending states in UI                    | High (queue, worker)  |

**A** keeps S7.2 (all fields non-null) true in the database itself, and codegen gives the web
form a typed error to render. **B** would behave the same, but the schema wouldn't document the
failure, and clients would parse `extensions`. **C** contradicts the requirement and forces
nullable `lat`/`long` everywhere. **D** also contradicts it, and would call Weatherstack outside
the mutation, which the brief forbids.

## Trade-off analysis

Rejecting a create when a third party is down reduces availability: a Weatherstack outage blocks
… 20 more lines truncated (3874 chars total)
```

I wrote three short ADRs, ran codegen, and made two commits on `chore/ai-harness`. `pnpm verify` passed before committing.

**ADRs**
- **ADR-0002, Yoga over Apollo Server:** Yoga lets tests run a full request inside the test process, with no open port, which suits the MSW-based Weatherstack tests. Apollo's advantages (federation and GraphOS, its hosted monitoring service) aren't used here. Switch if the API ever needs federation.
- **ADR-0003, Prisma over Drizzle:** Prisma's schema file reads like the SPEC's field table, the filters are one query object, and migrations come with drift checks. The downsides we actually hit are written down:
  - the trigram index needed a hand-edited migration;
  - Prisma 7 needs a driver adapter;
  - Prisma blocks `migrate reset` when an AI agent runs it;
  - npm's `latest` tag points at an 8.0 prerelease.
- **ADR-0004, Weatherstack failure policy:** creation fails with a typed `WeatherUnavailableError` and saves nothing. It compares four options:
  - a typed error (chosen);
  - a plain GraphQL error code;
  - saving the property without weather;
  - filling weather in later in the background.

  It also records the cost: when Weatherstack is down, nobody can create properties. It gives the reason for not retrying automatically: the free tier is about 100 calls a month, and errors like a bad key or used-up quota won't succeed on retry.

**Commits**
- `de89883 docs(adr): Yoga over Apollo Server, Prisma over Drizzle, weather failure policy`
- `787012c chore(api): scaffold GraphQL API, Prisma schema and test harness`
  - The API app, its first migration, the lockfile, the build-script approvals, the updated ESLint pattern and the scaffold reference.
  - No generated files, `.env` or `dist/` are in it.

**Still to do**
- `ai/sessions/` is still uncommitted.
- ADR-0003 has one follow-up for the S3 slice. The SPEC says `%` and `_` in the city filter must match literally (S3.3), and I haven't confirmed whether Prisma's `contains` escapes them.

Before coding, here's how I'll frame S5. I checked `apps/`: `apps/web` doesn't exist yet. That matters because S5.8 is UI and X4 is a Playwright flow.

**S5 plan: criterion → layer → the test that proves it**

| Criterion | Layer | Test |
|---|---|---|
| S5.1 one request, persist, return `CreatePropertySuccess` | service + repo + resolver | service unit test (counts calls on a fake client); integration test via `yoga.fetch` + MSW against postgres-test |
| S5.2 `query="<zip>, <state>, USA"`, `units=f`, key sent but never exposed or logged | weatherstack client | MSW handler asserts the query params; test asserts the key isn't in the result or error message |
| S5.3 validation (zip, trim, length, all fields reported, no WS call) | service (Zod) | one test per rule; MSW has no handler, so any request fails the test |
| S5.4 duplicate: before WS; case-sensitive; concurrent race | service + repo (unique constraint, Prisma error code `P2002`) | unit test (no WS call); integration test with two parallel creates → one success, one duplicate |
| S5.5 each `WeatherFailureReason`, nothing persisted | client (timeout, Zod parse) + service | one MSW test per reason: network, timeout, 500, `success:false`, malformed body, lat/lon out of range. Each asserts 0 rows |
| S5.6 country allow-list per state | service (config map) | tests: AZ + US country → OK; AZ + a non-US country → `LOCATION_MISMATCH`; PR + `Puerto Rico` → OK |
| S5.7 base URL and key from env | env.ts + server wiring | the scaffold already covers env parsing; client test uses the injected base URL |
| S5.8 form: validation, pending state, every union branch | **web** | Testing Library + MSW `graphql.mutation` |
| S7.1–S7.3, `Weather` field mapping (snake_case → camelCase) | resolver (`Property.weatherData`) | integration test selects every field |

**Order:** Weatherstack client (`AbortSignal.timeout(5000)`, Zod parse) → repository → service → resolver → tests → web → reviewer → `/verify`.

**Real Weatherstack call:** SPEC's open questions (and my notes) say to settle these now with one real call. I can't read `.env`, so I'd add a small `pnpm --filter api run weatherstack:probe "<query>"` script that uses the real client. It prints only `success`, `location.country`, `region` and `lat`/`lon`, never the key. You'd run it with `!` for `85268, AZ, USA` and `00901, PR, USA`. That's 2 of your ~100 monthly calls. It can also save the AZ response as the test fixture, so tests match the real response shape.

```
import { z } from 'zod';

/** Weatherstack sends coordinates as strings (SPEC S5.5); accept numbers too, reject non-numeric. */
const coordinate = (min: number, max: number) =>
  z
    .union([z.string().trim().min(1), z.number()])
    .transform(Number)
    .pipe(z.number().min(min).max(max));

/**
 * The `current` object. Fields the UI shows (S4.4) are required; the rest are optional so a
 * missing extra field doesn't block creation. Unknown keys are kept: it's stored as returned.
 */
export const WeatherstackCurrent = z.looseObject({
  observation_time: z.string(),
  temperature: z.number(),
  feelslike: z.number(),
  weather_descriptions: z.array(z.string()),
  weather_icons: z.array(z.string()),
  humidity: z.number(),
  wind_speed: z.number(),
  wind_dir: z.string(),
  weather_code: z.number().optional(),
  wind_degree: z.number().optional(),
  pressure: z.number().optional(),
  precip: z.number().optional(),
  cloudcover: z.number().optional(),
  uv_index: z.number().optional(),
  visibility: z.number().optional(),
  is_day: z.enum(['yes', 'no']).optional(),
});
export type WeatherstackCurrent = z.infer<typeof WeatherstackCurrent>;

export const WeatherstackLocation = z.looseObject({
  country: z.string(),
  region: z.string().optional(),
  name: z.string().optional(),
  lat: coordinate(-90, 90),
  lon: coordinate(-180, 180),
});
```

```
import { WeatherstackError, WeatherstackSuccess } from './schema.ts';

/** Why a lookup produced no usable data. Mirrors GraphQL `WeatherFailureReason` (SPEC S5.5). */
export type WeatherFailureReason =
  | 'NETWORK'
  | 'TIMEOUT'
  | 'HTTP_ERROR'
  | 'UPSTREAM_ERROR'
  | 'INVALID_RESPONSE'
  | 'LOCATION_MISMATCH';

export type WeatherLookup =
  | { ok: true; response: WeatherstackSuccess }
  | {
      ok: false;
      reason: Exclude<WeatherFailureReason, 'LOCATION_MISMATCH'>;
      /** For logs only. Never contains the access key; never sent to clients. */
      detail: string;
    };

export interface WeatherstackClient {
  /** Exactly one `/current` request; never throws. */
  current(query: string): Promise<WeatherLookup>;
}

  baseUrl: string;
  accessKey: string;
  timeoutMs?: number;
}

  baseUrl,
  accessKey,
  timeoutMs = 5000,
```

**🔧 Write** `~/Projects/covertree-properties/apps/api/scripts/weatherstack-probe.ts`

```
/**
 * Manual check against the REAL Weatherstack API (spends one call per query). Never used by tests.
 *
 *   pnpm --filter api run weatherstack:probe "85268, AZ, USA" [--save test/fixtures/weatherstack-current.json]
 *
 * Prints only non-secret fields. `--save` writes the parsed response as a test fixture.
 */
import { writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { parseArgs } from 'node:util';
import { readEnv } from '../src/env.ts';
import { createWeatherstackClient } from '../src/weatherstack/client.ts';

const { positionals, values } = parseArgs({
  allowPositionals: true,
  options: { save: { type: 'string' } },
});
const query = positionals[0];
if (!query) throw new Error('Usage: weatherstack:probe "<zip>, <state>, USA" [--save <path>]');

const env = readEnv();
const client = createWeatherstackClient({
  baseUrl: env.WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL,
  accessKey: env.WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY,
```

The fixture is clean: no key and no request URL, only the echoed query and units. It also tells us three things:

1. **The country string is unexpected.** `location.country` is `"USA United States of America"`, so the S5.6 row needs fixing. I'd accept both `"USA United States of America"` and `"United States of America"` for states and DC, in case the prefix is inconsistent across locations. Any other value stays `LOCATION_MISMATCH`.
2. **Weatherstack treated the zip as a place, not a postal code.** It reports `request.type: "City"` and rewrote the query as `"Fountain Hills, USA United States of America"`. It still resolved to the right place, and the free tier's `http://` base URL works. Both open questions for states are answered.
3. **`observation_time` is in UTC.** It says `10:35 AM` while the local time is `03:35`. The detail view (S4.4) should label it UTC. Descriptions also carry trailing spaces (`"Overcast "`), so the UI will trim them. The response has extra `astro` and `air_quality` objects, which get stored and aren't exposed.

I still need the Puerto Rico result before finishing the S5.6 table:

```

```

```bash
import { delay, http, HttpResponse } from 'msw';
import fixture from './fixtures/weatherstack-current.json' with { type: 'json' };

/** Matches WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL in vitest.config.ts. */
export const WEATHERSTACK_URL = 'http://weatherstack.test/current';
export const TEST_ACCESS_KEY = 'test-key';

/** Real response recorded by `weatherstack:probe "85268, AZ, USA"` (lat/lon are strings). */
export const weatherstackFixture = fixture;

type Body = Record<string, unknown>;

/** Handler that answers with `body` and records every request URL it receives. */
function respond(body: Body | (() => Response | Promise<Response>), requests: URL[] = []) {
  return http.get(WEATHERSTACK_URL, async ({ request }) => {
    requests.push(new URL(request.url));
    return typeof body === 'function' ? body() : HttpResponse.json(body);
  });
}

/** One MSW handler per Weatherstack outcome (SPEC S5.5). */
export const weatherstack = {
  ok: (requests?: URL[], body: Body = fixture) => respond(body, requests),
  withLocation: (location: Body, requests?: URL[]) =>
    respond({ ...fixture, location: { ...fixture.location, ...location } }, requests),
  networkError: (requests?: URL[]) => respond(() => HttpResponse.error(), requests),
  timeout: (requests?: URL[]) =>
    respond(async () => {
      await delay('infinite');
      return HttpResponse.json({});
    }, requests),
  httpError: (status = 500, requests?: URL[]) =>
    respond(() => new HttpResponse('upstream down', { status }), requests),
  successFalse: (requests?: URL[]) =>
    respond(
```

````
### Configure explicit HttpLink in ApolloClient

Source: https://github.com/apollographql/apollo-client/blob/main/docs/source/migrating/apollo-client-4-migration.mdx

Pass an explicit HttpLink instance to ApolloClient's link option instead of the removed top-level uri, headers, and credentials constructor options.

```typescript
import {
  ApolloClient,
  InMemoryCache,
  HttpLink,// [!code ++]
} from "@apollo/client/core";
const client = new ApolloClient({
  // ...
  link: new HttpLink({// [!code ++]
    uri: "https://example.com/graphql",
    credentials: "include",
    headers: { "x-custom-header": "value" },
  }),// [!code ++]
  // ...
});
```

--------------------------------

### Migrate URI, headers, and credentials to HttpLink

Source: https://github.com/apollographql/apollo-client/blob/main/docs/source/migrating/apollo-client-4-migration.mdx

Moves uri, headers, and credentials from ApolloClient options into a new HttpLink instance passed to the link option.

```ts
import { HttpLink } from "@apollo/client"; // [!code ++]

new ApolloClient({
  uri: "/graphql", // [!code --:5]
  credentials: "include",
  headers: {
    "x-custom-header"
  },
````

````
Source: https://github.com/shadcn-ui/ui/blob/shadcn@4.21.0/apps/v4/content/docs/installation/vite.mdx

```typescript
import path from "path"
import tailwindcss from "@tailwindcss/vite"
import react from "@vitejs/plugin-react"
import { defineConfig } from "vite"

// https://vite.dev/config/
export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [react(), tailwindcss()],
  resolve: {
    alias: {
      "@": path.resolve(__dirname, "./src"),
    },
  },
})
```
````

````
### Testing routes with createMemoryRouter in React Testing Library

Source: https://reactrouter.com/6.30.6/routers/create-memory-router

Use createMemoryRouter to configure an in-memory history stack with mock data and custom initial entries when writing tests.

```jsx
import {
  RouterProvider,
  createMemoryRouter,
} from "react-router-dom";
import * as React from "react";
import {
  render,
  waitFor,
  screen,
} from "@testing-library/react";
import "@testing-library/jest-dom";
import CalendarEvent from "./routes/event";

test("event route", async () => {
  const FAKE_EVENT = { name: "test event" };
  const routes = [
    {
      path: "/events/:id",
      element: <CalendarEvent />,
      loader: () => FAKE_EVENT,
    },
  ];

  const router = createMemoryRouter(routes, {
    initialEntries: ["/", "/events/123"],
    initialIndex: 1,
  });

  render(<RouterProvider router={router} />);

  await waitFor(() => screen.getByRole("heading"));
  expect(screen.getByRole("heading")).toHaveTextContent(
    FAKE_EVENT.name
````

```
# Plan: finish S5 slice (web scaffold + S5.8 form, review, verify)

## Context

`/graphql-slice S5` is mid-way. The user chose to scaffold `apps/web` in this slice (S5.8 is UI).
**Done (uncommitted, 49/49 API tests green, mutation-checked):** Weatherstack client + Zod schema,
probe script + real fixture, repository, create-property service, resolvers (union mapping, Weather
snake→camel), server wiring, unit + integration tests for S5.1–S5.7/S7. SPEC S5.6/S4.4 and ADR-0004
updated from the real probe (country is `USA United States of America` for states and PR).

**Current breakage:** `pnpm add` in `apps/web` failed because the `postinstall: graphql-codegen`
script ran before `apps/web/codegen.ts` existed. `apps/web/package.json` has no dependencies
(manifest not written); `apps/web/node_modules` is partially linked.

## Steps

### 1. Recover the web install
- Remove `"postinstall"` from `apps/web/package.json` temporarily, write `codegen.ts` first, then
  re-run the two `pnpm add` commands (runtime: react, react-dom, react-router, @apollo/client,
  graphql, rxjs; dev: vite, @vitejs/plugin-react, tailwindcss, @tailwindcss/vite, @types/react(-dom),
  @types/node, vitest, jsdom, @testing-library/{react,user-event,jest-dom}, msw,
  @graphql-codegen/{cli,client-preset}). Re-add `postinstall` once codegen runs.
- Add `apps/web/src/gql/` to `.gitignore` (already ignored by ESLint/Prettier).

### 2. Web scaffold (per `.claude/skills/graphql-slice/references/scaffold.md`)
- `tsconfig.json`: extends base, override `module: ESNext`, `moduleResolution: Bundler`,
  `jsx: react-jsx`, DOM libs, `paths: { "@/*": ["./src/*"] }`.
- `vite.config.ts`: react + tailwind plugins, `@` alias, `envDir: '../..'` (root `.env` has
  `VITE_GRAPHQL_URL`), vitest `environment: 'jsdom'`, `setupFiles: src/test/setup.ts`.
- `codegen.ts`: schema `../api/src/schema.graphql`, documents `src/**/*.{ts,tsx}`, preset `client`
  → `src/gql/`, `fragmentMasking: false`, `enumsAsConst: true` (runtime `USState` list for the
  select, no hand-written duplicate), `scalars: { DateTime: 'string' }`.
- `shadcn init -y -b radix -t vite`, then `shadcn add button input label card alert` (plus
  `native-select` if the registry has it; else a styled `<select>`). Verify flags with context7 docs
  already fetched (shadcn 4.21).
- `src/apollo.ts`: `new ApolloClient({ link: new HttpLink({ uri }), cache: new InMemoryCache() })`
  (Apollo 4: hooks from `@apollo/client/react`).
- `src/main.tsx` + `src/router.tsx`: `createBrowserRouter` routes `/` (placeholder until S1),
  `/properties/new` (S5), `/properties/:id` (placeholder until S4). React Router 8 is latest;
  docs are v7 — let the typecheck confirm import paths (`react-router` vs `react-router/dom`).
```

**🔧 Bash** — Write web codegen config, install deps, run codegen

```bash
cd ~/Projects/covertree-properties/apps/web
node -e 'const f="package.json",p=JSON.parse(require("fs").readFileSync(f));delete p.scripts.postinstall;require("fs").writeFileSync(f,JSON.stringify(p,null,2)+"\n")'
cat > codegen.ts <<'EOF'
import type { CodegenConfig } from '@graphql-codegen/cli';

const config: CodegenConfig = {
  schema: '../api/src/schema.graphql',
  documents: ['src/**/*.{ts,tsx}', '!src/gql/**'],
  ignoreNoDocuments: true,
  generates: {
    'src/gql/': {
      preset: 'client',
      presetConfig: { fragmentMasking: false },
      config: {
        useTypeImports: true,
        // Runtime object for the state <select>, generated from the schema rather than duplicated.
        enumsAsConst: true,
        scalars: { DateTime: 'string' },
      },
    },
  },
};

export default config;
EOF
pnpm add react react-dom react-router @apollo/client graphql rxjs 2>&1 | tail -3
pnpm add -D vite @vitejs/plugin-react tailwindcss @tailwindcss/vite @types/react @types/react-dom @types/node vitest jsdom @testing-library/react @testing-library/user-event @testing-library/jest-dom msw @graphql-codegen/cli @graphql-codegen/client-preset 2>&1 | tail -3
pnpm run codegen 2>&1 | tail -2; ls src/gql
```

```
import { graphql } from '@/gql';

export const CreatePropertyMutation = graphql(`
  mutation CreateProperty($input: CreatePropertyInput!) {
    createProperty(input: $input) {
      __typename
      ... on CreatePropertySuccess {
        property {
          id
        }
      }
      ... on InvalidInputError {
        message
        fieldErrors {
          field
          message
        }
      }
      ... on DuplicatePropertyError {
        message
        existingPropertyId
      }
      ... on WeatherUnavailableError {
        message
        reason
      }
    }
  }
`);

```

**🔧 Write** `~/Projects/covertree-properties/apps/web/src/features/properties/validate-property.ts`

```
/** Fields of the create form. `state` is '' until the user picks one. */
export interface PropertyFormValues {
  street: string;
  city: string;
  state: string;
  zipCode: string;
}

export type PropertyFormErrors = Partial<Record<keyof PropertyFormValues, string>>;

/**
 * Mirrors the server rules (SPEC S5.3) so users get instant feedback; the server stays the
 * source of truth and its InvalidInputError is shown the same way.
 */
export function validateProperty(values: PropertyFormValues): PropertyFormErrors {
  const errors: PropertyFormErrors = {};
  const street = values.street.trim();
  const city = values.city.trim();

  if (!street) errors.street = 'Street is required.';
  else if (street.length > 200) errors.street = 'Street must be at most 200 characters.';

  if (!city) errors.city = 'City is required.';
  else if (city.length > 100) errors.city = 'City must be at most 100 characters.';

  if (!values.state) errors.state = 'State is required.';

  if (!/^\d{5}$/.test(values.zipCode)) errors.zipCode = 'Zip code must be exactly 5 digits.';

  return errors;
}

```

<details><summary>mcp__context7__query-docs result (76 lines)</summary>

````
### Enum Configuration Options Comparison

Source: https://github.com/dotansimha/graphql-code-generator/blob/master/website/src/pages/docs/migration/operations-and-client-preset-from-5-0.mdx

This table compares previous and new configuration options for generating Enum types in GraphQL Code Generator, highlighting the consolidation into the `enumType` option.

```plaintext
| Enum type      | Examples                                                                     | Previous config              | New config                            |
| -------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------- |
| String literal | `type UserRole = 'Admin' \| 'Customer'`                                      | `{enumsAsTypes:true}`        | `{}` or `{enumType:'string-literal'}` |
| Const          | `export const UserRole = { Admin: 'ADMIN', Customer: 'CUSTOMER' } as const;` | `{enumsAsConst:true}`        | `{enumType:'const'}`                  |
| Native         | `export enum UserRole { Admin = 'ADMIN', Customer = 'CUSTOMER' };`           | `{}` or `{constEnums:false}` | `{enumType:'native'}`                 |
| Native const   | `export const enum UserRole { Admin = 'ADMIN', Customer = 'CUSTOMER' };`     | `{constEnums:true}`          | `{enumType:'native-const'}`           |
| Native numeric | `export enum UserRole { Admin = 0, Customer = 1 }`                           | `{numericEnums:true}`        | `{enumType:'native-numeric'}`         |
```

--------------------------------

### Configure GraphQL Codegen for Native Enum Types

Source: https://github.com/dotansimha/graphql-code-generator/blob/master/website/src/pages/docs/migration/apollo-tooling.mdx

Set `enumType: 'native'` in GraphQL Codegen configuration to produce native enum declarations matching apollo-tooling's output.

```typescript
const config: CodegenConfig = {
  ...
  generates: {
    ...
  }
  config: {
    ...
    namingConvention: 'keep',
    enumType: 'native',
    ...
  }
}
```

--------------------------------

````

**🔧 Bash** — Write Weatherstack stub and lint/format check

```bash
mkdir -p ~/Projects/covertree-properties/apps/web/e2e && cat > ~/Projects/covertree-properties/apps/web/e2e/weatherstack-stub.mjs <<'EOF'
// Local stand-in for Weatherstack so the running app never spends real API calls.
//   node e2e/weatherstack-stub.mjs            → serves the recorded Fountain Hills response
//   STUB_MODE=success-false node e2e/…       → HTTP 200 { success: false } (UPSTREAM_ERROR)
// Point the API at it with WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL=http://localhost:4999.
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { createServer } from 'node:http';

const port = Number(process.env.WEATHERSTACK_STUB_PORT ?? 4999);
const mode = process.env.STUB_MODE ?? 'ok';
const fixture = readFileSync(
  new URL('../../api/test/fixtures/weatherstack-current.json', import.meta.url),
  'utf8',
);
const failure = JSON.stringify({
  success: false,
  error: { code: 104, type: 'usage_limit_reached', info: 'Stubbed failure.' },
});

createServer((request, response) => {
  const { pathname } = new URL(request.url ?? '/', 'http://stub');
  if (pathname !== '/current') {
    response.writeHead(404).end();
    return;
  }
  response.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'application/json' });
  response.end(mode === 'success-false' ? failure : fixture);
}).listen(port, () => console.info(`Weatherstack stub (${mode}) on http://localhost:${port}`));
EOF
```

The reviewer is running in the background. I'll wait for its report before changing any S5 files, and run `/verify` after any fixes. So far:

- **API:** 49 tests pass, and I checked they catch bugs by deliberately breaking the code and watching them fail. They cover every `WeatherFailureReason`, duplicates (including two creates at once) and validation.
- **Web:** `apps/web` is scaffolded, the S5.8 form is built, and its 7 tests pass. The files are formatted and lint is clean across both apps.
- **Changes along the way:**
  - The two probe runs corrected SPEC S5.6: states and PR both report the country as "USA United States of America".
  - Codegen 6 renamed its enum setting, so the form's state list now comes from the schema via `enumType: 'const'`.
  - shadcn's init asked for a visual preset. I picked the default, Nova (Lucide icons, Geist font).

The reviewer's verdict is **NOT DONE**: 30 PASS, 3 UNTESTED, 1 FAIL. The FAIL is the "list includes it" part of S5.8, which can't be done until S1 exists. Most of the other findings are real and cheap to fix, so I'll fix them now:

- **Integer fields (#5):** Weatherstack's integer fields are only checked as numbers. A fractional `humidity` would be saved, and then every later read of that property would fail. That's the incomplete record S5.5 is meant to prevent.
- **Untested behaviour (#2–4):** the 5 s default timeout, the "one request per attempt, even on failure" rule, and the env → client wiring.
- **Low-severity issues (#7–12):**
  - a timeout while reading the body is reported as `INVALID_RESPONSE`;
  - a path in the base URL is dropped;
  - redaction misses a URL-encoded key;
  - odd numeric strings like `"0x10"` are accepted as coordinates;
  - the SDL calls `observationTime` local time, but it's UTC;
  - a few tests assert less than their names say.
- **Enforcement gap (#6):** the lint ban on raw `fetch` only covers three folders.

```bash
import type { PrismaClient } from './__generated__/prisma/client.ts';
import type { Env } from './env.ts';
import { createPropertyRepository } from './repositories/property.repository.ts';
import { createPropertyService } from './services/create-property.service.ts';
import type { Services } from './services/index.ts';
import { createWeatherstackClient } from './weatherstack/client.ts';

type WeatherstackEnv = Pick<Env, 'WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL' | 'WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY'>;

/**
 * Builds the services once (SPEC X1). The Weatherstack client is created here from env (S5.7)
 * and handed only to the create-property service; resolvers see nothing but `services`.
 */
export function createServices(env: WeatherstackEnv, prisma: PrismaClient): Services {
  const properties = createPropertyRepository(prisma);
  const weatherstack = createWeatherstackClient({
    baseUrl: env.WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL,
    accessKey: env.WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY,
  });
  return {
    createProperty: createPropertyService({ properties, weatherstack }),
  };
}
EOF
```

```bash
cd ~/Projects/covertree-properties/apps/api; python3 - <<'EOF'
import pathlib
p=pathlib.Path('src/weatherstack/client.ts'); s=p.read_text()

rep("""const isTimeout = (error: unknown) => error instanceof DOMException && error.name === 'TimeoutError';
""","""const isTimeout = (error: unknown) => error instanceof DOMException && error.name === 'TimeoutError';

/** Rejects with the signal's reason once it aborts, so no await can outlive the timeout. */
const rejectOnAbort = (signal: AbortSignal) =>
  new Promise<never>((_resolve, reject) => {
    if (signal.aborted) reject(signal.reason);
    signal.addEventListener('abort', () => reject(signal.reason), { once: true });
  });
""")
rep("""      let response: Response;
      try {
        response = await fetch(url, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(timeoutMs) });""","""      const signal = AbortSignal.timeout(timeoutMs);
      let response: Response;
      try {
        response = await fetch(url, { signal });""")
rep("        body = await response.json();","        body = await Promise.race([response.json(), rejectOnAbort(signal)]);")
p.write_text(s)
EOF
```

**🔧 Bash** — Apply small review fixes and favicon, rerun full verify

```bash
cd ~/Projects/covertree-properties; python3 - <<'EOF'
import pathlib
def sub(path, pairs):
    p=pathlib.Path(path); s=p.read_text()
    for a,b in pairs:
        assert a in s, (path,a[:50]); s=s.replace(a,b)
    p.write_text(s)
sub('apps/api/src/env.ts',[("""export function readEnv(source: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): Env {
  loadRootEnv();""","""export function readEnv(source: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): Env {
  // Only the real process env is backed by the repo-root .env; explicit sources (tests) stay pure.
  if (source === process.env) loadRootEnv();""")])
sub('eslint.config.mjs',[("'Resolvers must not use the Weatherstack client; it is injected into services via context.'",
 "'Resolvers must not use the Weatherstack client; src/container.ts injects it into the create-property service.'")])
sub('apps/web/index.html',[('    <title>Covertree properties</title>','    <link rel="icon" type="image/svg+xml" href="/favicon.svg" />\n    <title>Covertree properties</title>')])
EOF
mkdir -p apps/web/public && cat > apps/web/public/favicon.svg <<'EOF'
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 0 32 32"><path d="M16 3 4 29h24L16 3Z" fill="#00806a"/><path d="M16 12 10 25h12l-6-13Z" fill="#fff"/></svg>
EOF
grep -q '^.playwright-mcp/' .gitignore || printf '.playwright-mcp/\n' >> .gitignore
grep -q 'playwright-mcp' .prettierignore || printf '.playwright-mcp\n' >> .prettierignore
grep -q "playwright-mcp" eslint.config.mjs || sed -i '' "s#      'test-results/\*\*',#      'test-results/**',\n      '.playwright-mcp/**',#" eslint.config.mjs
S=/private/tmp/claude-501/-~-Projects-covertree-properties/9a6944a8-8343-4087-bfe9-7653041fe6fe/scratchpad
pnpm run verify > $S/verify3.log 2>&1; echo "verify exit $?"; grep -E "Tests |error|✖|\[warn\]|×" $S/verify3.log | head
```

S5 is built end to end but not fully done. One piece of S5.8, the property list showing the new record, can only be finished once S1 adds a list. Everything else passed the reviewer on its second pass and the full `/verify` gate. Nothing is committed.

## Verify: PASS (e2e not built yet)
| Stage | Result | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| database | ✅ | postgres and postgres-test healthy, no pending migrations |
| format · lint · typecheck | ✅ | |
| test | ✅ | api 64/64 (unit + integration against postgres-test), web 8/8 |
| build · e2e | ✅ / ⏭ | both apps build; no e2e exists yet (X4 comes after S1/S3/S4/S6) |
| UI smoke | ✅ | local stub, no real Weatherstack calls |

The smoke run in the browser covered four paths:
- **Invalid zip:** the form showed the error and sent nothing.
- **Valid create:** it opened the new property's page, and the database row has lat/long and 79°F.
- **Same address again:** a duplicate message with a link to the existing property.
- **Stub stopped:** "Weather data unavailable, property not created", with the values kept, no row saved and no key in the API log.

It also caught a real layout bug: the State label dropped when the zip error appeared. That's fixed. Screenshots are in `.playwright-mcp/`, which I've gitignored. One smoke-test property is left in your dev database.

**Review:** the first pass found 3 untested criteria and 12 smaller issues. After fixes, it found 33 pass and 0 untested. Three fixes mattered most:
- **Fractional numbers from Weatherstack** (e.g. humidity `42.5`) are now rejected. Before, the record would have been saved and then failed on every read.
- **A stalled response body now times out.** Before, a slow Weatherstack could hang the request forever. A new test found this.
- **The env → client wiring is now tested** and lives in one place, `src/container.ts`.

**What the probes changed:** Weatherstack reports every US location as `USA United States of America`, including Puerto Rico. The SPEC's per-territory table would have rejected all of them, so S5.6 now uses one US list. `observation_time` turned out to be UTC, and the SPEC and schema now say so.

**Still open:**
- **S5.8 list refresh:** blocked on S1, and the reviewer's only remaining FAIL. S1 has to add `refetchQueries` to this form's mutation, with a test.
- **Detail page:** a placeholder until S4.
- **Not fixed from the review:**
  - The redaction of a URL-encoded key has no test.
  - A body that times out is left open until garbage collection; it has no effect on results.
  - The failure-reason list is written by hand in two places. If they drift apart, only the resolver's type check notices, and only in one direction.
- **Setup choices:** Codegen 6 renamed its enum setting, so the state list now comes straight from the schema. For shadcn's visual preset I picked the default, Nova.

I'd split this into three commits:
1. `feat(api): S5 createProperty with Weatherstack enrichment`
2. `feat(web): scaffold web app and S5 create-property form`
3. `docs: S5.6 verified against real Weatherstack`

Should I make them?

```
- **S2.2** Given properties created in order A, B, C: `ASC` returns A, B, C; `DESC` and the

- **S2.3** Properties with identical `createdAt` are ordered by `id` in the same direction, so
```

```bash
import type { createApp } from '../src/app.ts';

export interface GraphQLResponse<TData> {
  data?: TData;
  errors?: { message: string; extensions?: { code?: string } }[];
}

/** POSTs an operation through Yoga in-process (ADR-0002); returns raw text and parsed body. */
export async function executeOperation<TData>(
  app: ReturnType<typeof createApp>,
  query: string,
  variables: Record<string, unknown> = {},
) {
  const response = await app.fetch('http://api.test/graphql', {
    method: 'POST',
    headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' },
    body: JSON.stringify({ query, variables }),
  });
  const text = await response.text();
  return { text, body: JSON.parse(text) as GraphQLResponse<TData> };
}
EOF
```

<details><summary>Bash result (4 lines)</summary>

```
- The resolver importing the Weatherstack client "just this once" is a lint error by design.
- `throw new GraphQLError(...)` from `graphql` gets masked to `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR` (graphql 17
  wraps it via `cause`, and Yoga 5 doesn't unwrap that). Use `createGraphQLError` from `graphql-yoga`
  for `BAD_USER_INPUT`, and assert `extensions.code` in a test.
```

  - Your dev database now has 2 test properties.

**What was built**
- **API:** `Query.properties` sorts by creation date, then by `id` in the same direction when timestamps tie (S2.3). `orderBy: {}` also counts as newest first. A non-empty `filter` is rejected with `BAD_USER_INPUT` until S3, so it's never silently ignored.
- **Web:** `/` is now the list, with loading, error-with-retry and empty states, plus the sort toggle. The list uses `cache-and-network` so it's fresh after a create. I confirmed that removing that line makes the S5.8 test fail.

**Found along the way**
- **Masked errors:** with graphql 17, a `new GraphQLError` thrown in a resolver reaches the client as `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR`. Yoga's `createGraphQLError` gets through intact. I added this to the graphql-slice skill's pitfalls, since S3 will hit it again.
- **Refactors:** the create service now depends only on the two repository methods it uses. A shared GraphQL request helper (`test/graphql.ts`) replaces the copy in the create test.

The reviewer says **DONE**: 10 PASS, 0 untested, 0 fail. It confirmed the `cache-and-network` reasoning. It raised three minor points, all quick, so I'll fix them before suggesting commits:
- `filter: { zipCode: "" }` slips past the temporary guard and returns the unfiltered list.
- The schema's `createdAt: SortDirection! = DESC` rejects an explicit `null`, while the SPEC writes it as nullable.
- The list integration test wires services by hand instead of going through the production `createServices`.

**The reviewer's three points, now fixed**

| S3.10 no match → `[]`; UI shows "no matches" + clear | repository + web | integration + web test |

```bash
import { useState, type FormEvent } from 'react';
import { Button } from '@/components/ui/button';
import { Input } from '@/components/ui/input';
import { Label } from '@/components/ui/label';
import { NativeSelect, NativeSelectOption } from '@/components/ui/native-select';
import { USState, type PropertyFilter } from '@/gql/graphql';

const STATES = Object.values(USState);

export interface FilterValues {
  city: string;
  zipCode: string;
  state: string;
}

export const NO_FILTER: FilterValues = { city: '', zipCode: '', state: '' };

/** Blank fields mean "any" (S3.4); the server trims and validates again. */
export function toPropertyFilter({ city, zipCode, state }: FilterValues): PropertyFilter {
  return {
    city: city.trim() || null,
    zipCode: zipCode.trim() || null,
    state: (state as USState) || null,
  };
}

export const isFiltering = (values: FilterValues) =>
  Object.values(toPropertyFilter(values)).some((value) => value !== null);
```

S3 is built and passes both the full gate and a browser smoke test. The reviewer is still running in the background; I'll act on its report before suggesting commits. Nothing is committed yet.

**Checks so far**
- `pnpm verify` passes: 99 API tests (was 77) and 19 web tests (was 13).
- Browser smoke test, against the stub:
  - I created a San Juan, PR property.
  - The city filter `fountain` hid it.
  - `fountain` + `PR` showed the "no matches" state.
  - "Clear filters" reset the inputs and brought all three properties back.
  - The console stayed clean.
- Your dev database now has 3 test properties.

**Prisma treats `%` and `_` as wildcards.** A probe against the test database showed that `contains` doesn't escape them: `Fo%` and `n_H` both matched "Fountain Hills". S3.3 requires literal matching, so the repository now escapes `\`, `%` and `_` itself. I checked the tests by removing the escaping (4 failed) and removing case-insensitivity (6 failed). That settles ADR-0003's open action item.

**What was built**
- **API:** the service trims each filter field, treats blanks as "any", and checks that a zip has exactly 5 digits. A bad zip returns `BAD_USER_INPUT` with a clear message. S1's temporary filter rejection and its tests are gone.
- **Web:** City, Zip and State inputs with Apply (or Enter) and Clear. A partial zip is caught before any request is sent. Filters stay applied when you change the sort order, and "no matches" is shown separately from "no properties yet".
- **SPEC:** S3.4 and S3.6 are reworded so a blank zip means no filter, as you decided.

**Fixes after the review**

<details><summary>Read result (287 lines)</summary>

```
1	# SPEC — Covertree property records
2	
3	Source: Covertree "AI Software Engineer" assessment brief plus product decisions recorded
4	below. This spec is the contract the `reviewer` agent checks against; story IDs (`S1`…`S7`)
5	and criterion IDs (`S3.2`) are stable — reference them in commits, tests and reviews.
6	
7	## Problem statement
8	
9	Covertree needs to manage records of US residential properties through a GraphQL API with a
10	React UI. Each property must be enriched with the current weather and coordinates of its
11	location at the moment it is created, so records carry location data the user never has to
12	enter by hand. A property without that data is incomplete, so it must never be stored.
13	
14	## Goals
15	
16	1. Every user story S1–S7 is implemented end to end (API + UI) and covered by automated tests
17	   named after the criteria below.
18	2. Weatherstack is called **only** inside the `createProperty` mutation — never on reads,
19	   deletes, or in tests.
20	3. No property is ever persisted without Weatherstack weather, `lat` and `long`; every failure
21	   mode reaches the client as a **typed** error in the schema.
22	4. The GraphQL schema is the single contract: server resolvers and client documents are typed
23	   from it by codegen, with zero hand-written duplicate types.
24	5. A reviewer can run the whole app from the README in ≤ 3 commands (plus `.env`).
25	
26	## Non-goals
27	
28	- **Authentication / multi-tenancy** — not in the brief; every user sees all properties.
29	- **Updating a property** — the brief lists create/read/delete only; weather is a creation
30	  snapshot, so edits would make it misleading.
31	- **Refreshing weather after creation** — the brief forbids calling Weatherstack outside creation.
32	- **Address autocomplete / geocoding services** — lat/long come from the Weatherstack response.
33	- **Pagination** — P2; the dataset in scope is small. The list API takes input objects so it
34	  can be added without breaking the contract.
35	- **Non-US addresses** — the brief assumes all properties are in the United States.
36	
37	## Decisions
38	
39	| Topic                  | Decision                                                                                                                                                              |
40	| ---------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
```

```
1	import { z } from 'zod';

3	/** Weatherstack sends coordinates as strings (SPEC S5.5); accept numbers too, reject non-numeric. */
4	const coordinate = (min: number, max: number) =>
5	  z
6	    .union([z.string().trim().min(1), z.number()])
7	    .transform(Number)
8	    .pipe(z.number().min(min).max(max));
9
```

```
1	import { z } from 'zod';
2	import { loadRootEnv } from './load-env.ts';
3	
4	const Env = z.object({
5	  DATABASE_URL: z.url(),
6	  // Free tier is HTTP-only (SPEC open questions), so the base URL is configurable.
7	  WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL: z.url().default('http://api.weatherstack.com'),
8	  WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY: z.string().min(1),
9	  PORT: z.coerce.number().int().positive().default(4000),
10	});
11	
12	export type Env = z.infer<typeof Env>;
13	
14	export function readEnv(source: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): Env {
15	  loadRootEnv();
16	  const parsed = Env.safeParse(source);
17	  if (!parsed.success) {
18	    // Report variable names only; values may be secrets (SPEC S5.2).
19	    const names = parsed.error.issues.map((issue) => issue.path.join('.')).join(', ');
20	    throw new Error(`Invalid or missing environment variables: ${names}. See .env.example.`);
21	  }
22	  return parsed.data;
23	}
24
```

```
1	/** Fields of the create form. `state` is '' until the user picks one. */
2	export interface PropertyFormValues {
3	  street: string;
4	  city: string;
5	  state: string;
6	  zipCode: string;
7	}
8	
9	export type PropertyFormErrors = Partial<Record<keyof PropertyFormValues, string>>;
10	
11	/**
12	 * Mirrors the server rules (SPEC S5.3) so users get instant feedback; the server stays the
13	 * source of truth and its InvalidInputError is shown the same way.
14	 */
15	export function validateProperty(values: PropertyFormValues): PropertyFormErrors {
16	  const errors: PropertyFormErrors = {};
17	  const street = values.street.trim();
18	  const city = values.city.trim();
19	
20	  if (!street) errors.street = 'Street is required.';
21	  else if (street.length > 200) errors.street = 'Street must be at most 200 characters.';
22	
23	  if (!city) errors.city = 'City is required.';
24	  else if (city.length > 100) errors.city = 'City must be at most 100 characters.';
25	
26	  if (!values.state) errors.state = 'State is required.';
27	
28	  if (!/^\d{5}$/.test(values.zipCode)) errors.zipCode = 'Zip code must be exactly 5 digits.';
29	
30	  return errors;
31	}
```

I've sent the S5 review to the calling agent. The verdict is **NOT DONE**: 30 criteria pass, 3 work in code but have no test, and 1 fails. I couldn't run anything, so this is based on reading the code and tests only.

**What fails:**
- **S5.8, "the list includes it" (the deferral you already know about).** Nothing refreshes the list after a create yet: the mutation only asks for the new property's `id` and doesn't refetch or update the cache. Once S1 adds the list, a cached list won't show the new property unless S1 also changes this mutation (`apps/web/src/features/properties/create-property-form.tsx:32,47`).

**Code looks right but no test pins it:**
- **S5.7:** nothing tests that `WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL` and `WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY` actually reach the client (`apps/api/src/env.ts:7-8`, `apps/api/src/server.ts:12-15`). Every test builds the client with hard-coded values.
- **S5.5, no retry:** no failure-path test checks that exactly one request was made. Request counts are only checked on success.
- **S5.5, 5-second timeout:** the default at `apps/api/src/weatherstack/client.ts:40` is never checked, because tests shorten it to 50 or 100 ms.

**Main risk found:**
- `humidity`, `weatherCode`, `windDegree` and `cloudcover` are integers in the GraphQL schema, but the Weatherstack response is only checked for "is a number". A decimal value would be saved and then break when the property is read. Since `humidity` is required, the whole property would error, which is the incomplete-but-saved record S5.5 is meant to prevent. The checks are in `apps/api/src/weatherstack/schema.ts`.

**Passing:**
- All six failure reasons return the typed weather error in `data`, save nothing, and each has a test.
- Validation and the duplicate check run before the Weatherstack call.
- The concurrent-create race is tested against the real test database.
- Layering is enforced by lint, and tests can't reach the real Weatherstack.

**Lower-severity findings in the full report:**
- **Where the client is injected:** SPEC X1 says through the GraphQL context, but it is passed straight into the create service. Resolvers still can't reach it, so I marked X1 as passing with a note.
- **Raw `fetch` lint rule:** it only covers resolvers, services and repositories, so a raw call elsewhere in `apps/api/src` wouldn't be flagged. Nothing does that today.
- **Timeout while reading the body:** it is reported as `INVALID_RESPONSE` instead of `TIMEOUT`.
- **Base URL:** any path in `WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL` is dropped.
- **Hiding the key in logs:** only the raw key is replaced, not its URL-encoded form.
- **Coordinates:** unusual numeric strings like `"0x10"` are accepted.
- **`observationTime` description:** `schema.graphql:96` calls it local time, but it is UTC. Worth fixing before S4.
- **Test gaps:**
  - The S7.1 test only sends `lat` and `long`, though its name also mentions `weatherData`.
  - The database-level duplicate test doesn't check that no second Weatherstack call was made.

Main files: `apps/api/src/weatherstack/client.ts`, `apps/api/src/weatherstack/schema.ts`, `apps/api/src/services/create-property.service.ts`, `apps/api/src/env.ts`, `apps/api/test/create-property.int.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/features/properties/create-property-form.tsx`.

```
3	/** Weatherstack sends coordinates as strings (SPEC S5.5); accept numbers too, reject non-numeric. */
4	const coordinate = (min: number, max: number) =>
5	  z
6	    .union([z.string().regex(/^-?\d+(\.\d+)?$/), z.number()])
7	    .transform(Number)
8	    .pipe(z.number().min(min).max(max));
```
