```
1	import { z } from 'zod';
2	import { loadRootEnv } from './load-env.ts';
3	
4	const Env = z.object({
5	  DATABASE_URL: z.url(),
6	  // Free tier is HTTP-only (SPEC open questions), so the base URL is configurable.
7	  WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL: z.url().default('http://api.weatherstack.com'),
8	  WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY: z.string().min(1),
9	  PORT: z.coerce.number().int().positive().default(4000),
10	});
11	
12	export type Env = z.infer<typeof Env>;
13	
14	export function readEnv(source: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): Env {
15	  loadRootEnv();
16	  const parsed = Env.safeParse(source);
17	  if (!parsed.success) {
18	    // Report variable names only; values may be secrets (SPEC S5.2).
19	    const names = parsed.error.issues.map((issue) => issue.path.join('.')).join(', ');
20	    throw new Error(`Invalid or missing environment variables: ${names}. See .env.example.`);
21	  }
22	  return parsed.data;
23	}
```

I re-reviewed S5 after the fixes and sent the updated report to the coordinator. The verdict is still NOT DONE, but only because of the S5.8 list refresh you've deferred to S1: 33 checks pass, none are untested, and that one fails. I couldn't run anything, so this comes from reading the code and tests.

**Still failing:**
- **S5.8, "the list includes it":** the mutation still asks only for the new property's `id` and doesn't refetch or update the cache. When S1 adds the list, it needs to change this mutation too, with a test.

**Now passing:** I read the code and tests behind each of the earlier findings #2–#12, and all are fixed.
- **S5.7:** tested through `readEnv` and `createServices`, and through a GraphQL call that checks where the request went and which key it sent.
- **No retry:** every failure-path test now asserts exactly one request.
- **5-second timeout:** the default is tested, and a stalled response body is now reported as `TIMEOUT`.
- **Validation:** decimal humidity, hex and exponent coordinates are rejected.
- **Other fixes:** the fetch lint rule now covers all of `apps/api/src`, and the base-URL path, S7.1 and web over-length tests are in.

**Remaining low-severity notes:**
- **Tests load the real `.env`:** `readEnv()` loads the repo-root `.env` on every call, and tests now call it. The fake Weatherstack values in `apps/api/vitest.config.ts` win and MSW blocks the real host, so nothing leaks today. But if those vitest entries were removed, the real key would reach the S5.7 test.
- **Encoded-key hiding is untested:** the test key has no characters that need encoding, so removing that line wouldn't fail any test.
- **Old lint message:** the resolver rule's message at `eslint.config.mjs:65` still says the client is injected "via context".
- **Lint gap:** the `fetch` ban doesn't catch `globalThis.fetch(...)`. Nothing uses it today.
- **Stream left open:** when the body-read timeout fires, the response body isn't cancelled. It doesn't affect the result.
- **Unchanged, as you said:** the failure-reason list in `client.ts` is still written by hand instead of coming from the generated GraphQL types.

<details><summary>🧩 reviewer — Review S3 filtering (<code>a708e5ea683350a31</code>)</summary>

#### 🧑 User

Review story S3 (filter by city, zip code, state) against docs/SPEC.md: all criteria S3.1–S3.10, plus X1–X2, and check that S1/S2 behaviour did not regress. Work is uncommitted in the working tree.

Context: in this slice the user resolved a SPEC conflict — a blank (empty/whitespace) zipCode filter now means "no constraint" like blank city; S3.4 and S3.6 were reworded accordingly (see `git diff docs/SPEC.md`). The filter UI applies on an Apply button (user's choice), with client-side zip validation. An empirical probe showed Prisma's `contains` does NOT escape `%`/`_`, so the repository escapes them (escapeLike).

```
1	# SPEC — Covertree property records
2	
3	Source: Covertree "AI Software Engineer" assessment brief plus product decisions recorded
4	below. This spec is the contract the `reviewer` agent checks against; story IDs (`S1`…`S7`)
5	and criterion IDs (`S3.2`) are stable — reference them in commits, tests and reviews.
6	
7	## Problem statement
8	
9	Covertree needs to manage records of US residential properties through a GraphQL API with a
10	React UI. Each property must be enriched with the current weather and coordinates of its
11	location at the moment it is created, so records carry location data the user never has to
12	enter by hand. A property without that data is incomplete, so it must never be stored.
13	
14	## Goals
15	
16	1. Every user story S1–S7 is implemented end to end (API + UI) and covered by automated tests
17	   named after the criteria below.
18	2. Weatherstack is called **only** inside the `createProperty` mutation — never on reads,
19	   deletes, or in tests.
20	3. No property is ever persisted without Weatherstack weather, `lat` and `long`; every failure
21	   mode reaches the client as a **typed** error in the schema.
22	4. The GraphQL schema is the single contract: server resolvers and client documents are typed
23	   from it by codegen, with zero hand-written duplicate types.
24	5. A reviewer can run the whole app from the README in ≤ 3 commands (plus `.env`).
25	
26	## Non-goals
27	
28	- **Authentication / multi-tenancy** — not in the brief; every user sees all properties.
29	- **Updating a property** — the brief lists create/read/delete only; weather is a creation
30	  snapshot, so edits would make it misleading.
31	- **Refreshing weather after creation** — the brief forbids calling Weatherstack outside creation.
32	- **Address autocomplete / geocoding services** — lat/long come from the Weatherstack response.
33	- **Pagination** — P2; the dataset in scope is small. The list API takes input objects so it
34	  can be added without breaking the contract.
35	- **Non-US addresses** — the brief assumes all properties are in the United States.
36	
37	## Decisions
38	
39	| Topic                  | Decision                                                                                                                                                              |
40	| ---------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
… 247 more lines truncated (19114 chars total)
```

```
15	export function createServices(env: WeatherstackEnv, prisma: PrismaClient): Services {
16	  const properties = createPropertyRepository(prisma);
17	  const weatherstack = createWeatherstackClient({
18	    baseUrl: env.WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL,
19	    accessKey: env.WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY,
20	  });
21	  return {
22	    createProperty: createPropertyService({ properties, weatherstack }),
23	    listProperties: listPropertiesService({ properties }),
24	  };
25	}
```

I sent the review to the calling agent. The verdict is DONE: all 19 criteria I checked pass, with none untested and none failing.

I only read the code; I didn't run anything.

- **S3.1–S3.10:** each one has code plus an API test named after it in `apps/api/test/filter-properties.int.test.ts`. Most also have a UI test in `apps/web/src/features/properties/property-filters.test.tsx`.
- **`%`/`_` escaping (S3.3):** the tests would catch it if escaping broke, because a bare `%` or `_` would then return every row.
- **Blank zip (S3.4) and bad zip (S3.6):** a blank or whitespace zip adds no filter. A non-blank zip that isn't exactly 5 digits returns a `BAD_USER_INPUT` error, not an empty list.
- **X1 (layering):** the ESLint config enforces it, with no rule-disabling comments outside generated code. Only the create-property service gets the Weatherstack client.
- **X2 (no real Weatherstack in tests):** MSW is set to fail on any unhandled request in both the API and web tests, with a fake URL and key. Each weather failure reason has a test.
- **S1/S2 regression:** nothing broke. The existing list and sort tests still hold, and a new test checks that the filter survives a sort toggle.

Minor findings, none blocking:
1. **E2E has no filter step.** The Playwright tests in `apps/web/e2e` never filter, but the happy path required by X4 (outside this review) includes one. S4 and S6 aren't built yet, so that path is unfinished anyway.
2. **S3.9 test ignores order.** It sorts the results before comparing, so it doesn't check that the no-filter list comes back newest-first. The S2 tests already pin that order on the same code path.
3. **Backslash escape untested.** The escaping also covers backslashes in city filters, but no test uses one.
4. **One SPEC table row not updated.** The "Zip filter" row in `docs/SPEC.md` (line 44) still says the value must be exactly 5 digits and doesn't mention the blank case. The reworded S3.4/S3.6 and the "Filter semantics" row already cover it, so rewording that row would just avoid confusion.

```
9	type WeatherstackEnv = Pick<Env, 'WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL' | 'WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY'>;

11	/**
12	 * Builds the services once (SPEC X1). The Weatherstack client is created here from env (S5.7)
13	 * and handed only to the create-property service; resolvers see nothing but `services`.
14	 */
15	export function createServices(env: WeatherstackEnv, prisma: PrismaClient): Services {
16	  const properties = createPropertyRepository(prisma);
17	  const weatherstack = createWeatherstackClient({
18	    baseUrl: env.WEATHERSTACK_BASE_URL,
19	    accessKey: env.WEATHERSTACK_ACCESS_KEY,
20	  });
21	  return {
22	    createProperty: createPropertyService({ properties, weatherstack }),
23	    listProperties: listPropertiesService({ properties }),
24	  };
25	}
```

I've sent the review to the calling agent. All 10 criteria pass (S1.1–S1.3, S2.1–S2.4, the S5.8 list clause, X1, X2), so S1 and S2 are done. I couldn't run anything, so every verdict comes from reading the code and tests.

**Your two decisions:**
- **Rejecting `filter` until S3:** the approach is fine, but the guard (`property.resolvers.ts:22`) has a gap. `filter: { zipCode: "" }` counts as empty, so it's silently ignored and returns the full list. That breaks the "never silently ignored" goal and S3.6, which says a zip that isn't 5 digits must get `BAD_USER_INPUT`. Only the `state` filter has a test; `city` and `zipCode` don't.
- **`cache-and-network` for S5.8:** the reasoning is right. The list isn't on screen during create, so a named refetch would have nothing to refresh. The S5.8 test would fail if the policy were the default, so it really pins the behaviour. The cost is a network request every time the list opens. S6 delete will also need its own cache eviction or test, because this policy briefly shows the deleted row.

**Smaller findings:**
- **S2.1 doesn't match the spec:** the schema makes `orderBy.createdAt` required with a default of DESC, but the SPEC has it nullable. So sending `orderBy: { createdAt: null }` returns a validation error instead of defaulting to DESC. Leaving `orderBy` out, or passing `{}`, works and is tested.
- **S1.3 test is weaker than it looks:** it builds the services by hand, not through `container.ts`. It proves the list service makes no outbound request, but mis-wiring the Weatherstack client into it would be caught by the type and lint rules, not by this test.
- **S5.8 test depends on a stub:** it waits for the new id on the placeholder detail page, so it will need updating when S4 lands.
- **List view:** it doesn't show lat/long. S1.1 only requires them in the API, and the integration test covers that.

X1 has no runtime test; the ESLint layering rules enforce it through `pnpm verify`. There are no `eslint-disable` comments except in generated files.
